{"parentUuid":"2aace449-fea4-4454-bc9e-fb77acd9c8a8","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a4d41d308033b3233","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- heartbeat-protocol: Claude node contract for DebateAI Graph Spine v2. The Main Orchestrator (Claude-Router seat) contract; thin loader over the repo spine. All agent launches use /goal.\n- commit-commands:clean_gone: Cleans up all git branches marked as [gone] (branches that have been deleted on the remote but still exist locally), including removing associated worktrees.\n- commit-commands:commit-push-pr: Commit, push, and open a PR\n- commit-commands:commit: Create a git commit\n- pdf-viewer:annotate: Collaboratively annotate a PDF — propose markup, review together, iterate\n- pdf-viewer:fill-form: Fill PDF form fields interactively with live visual feedback\n- pdf-viewer:open: Open a PDF in the interactive viewer\n- pdf-viewer:sign: Place a signature or initials image on a PDF\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- mattpocock-skills:diagnosing-bugs: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Use when the user says \"diagnose\"/\"debug this\", or reports something broken/throwing/failing/slow.\n- mattpocock-skills:tdd: Test-driven development. Use when the user wants to build features or fix bugs test-first, mentions \"red-green-refactor\", or wants integration tests.\n- mattpocock-skills:prototype: Build a throwaway prototype to answer a design question. Use when the user wants to sanity-check whether a state model or logic feels right, or explore what a UI should look like.\n- mattpocock-skills:research: Investigate a question against high-trust primary sources and capture the findings as a Markdown file in the repo. Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent.\n- mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when the user wants to pin down domain terminology or a ubiquitous language, record an architectural decision, or when another skill needs to maintain the domain model.\n- mattpocock-skills:codebase-design: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary.\n- mattpocock-skills:code-review: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \"review since X\".\n- mattpocock-skills:resolving-merge-conflicts: Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\n- mattpocock-skills:grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\n- superpowers:brainstorming: You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation.\n- superpowers:dispatching-parallel-agents: Use when facing 2+ independent tasks that can be worked on without shared state or sequential dependencies\n- superpowers:executing-plans: Use when you have a written implementation plan to execute in a separate session with review checkpoints\n- superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch: Use when implementation is complete, all tests pass, and you need to decide how to integrate the work\n- superpowers:receiving-code-review: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\n- superpowers:requesting-code-review: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n- superpowers:subagent-driven-development: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n- superpowers:systematic-debugging: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n- superpowers:test-driven-development: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n- superpowers:using-git-worktrees: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback\n- superpowers:using-superpowers: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions\n- superpowers:verification-before-completion: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n- superpowers:writing-plans: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code\n- superpowers:writing-skills: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment\n- pdf-viewer:view-pdf: Interactive PDF viewer. Use when the user wants to open, show, or view a PDF and collaborate on it visually — annotate, highlight, stamp, fill form fields, place signature/initials, or review markup together. Not for summarization or text extraction (use native Read instead).\n- productivity:memory-management: Two-tier memory system that makes Claude a true workplace collaborator. Decodes shorthand, acronyms, nicknames, and internal language so Claude understands requests like a colleague would. CLAUDE.md for working memory, memory/ directory for the full knowledge base.\n- productivity:start: Initialize the productivity system and open the dashboard. Use when setting up the plugin for the first time, bootstrapping working memory from your existing task list, or decoding the shorthand (nicknames, acronyms, project codenames) you use in your todos.\n- productivity:task-management: Simple task management using a shared TASKS.md file. Reference this when the user asks about their tasks, wants to add/complete tasks, or needs help tracking commitments.\n- productivity:update: Sync tasks and refresh memory from your current activity. Use when pulling new assignments from your project tracker into TASKS.md, triaging stale or overdue tasks, filling memory gaps for unknown people or projects, or running a comprehensive scan to catch todos buried in chat and email.\n- cowork-plugin-management:cowork-plugin-customizer: Customize a Claude Code plugin for a specific organization's tools and workflows. Use when: customize plugin, set up plugin, configure plugin, tailor plugin, adjust plugin settings, customize plugin connectors, customize plugin skill, tweak plugin, modify plugin configuration.\n- cowork-plugin-management:create-cowork-plugin: Guide users through creating a new plugin from scratch in a cowork session. Use when users want to create a plugin, build a plugin, make a new plugin, develop a plugin, scaffold a plugin, start a plugin from scratch, or design a plugin. This skill requires Cowork mode with access to the outputs directory for delivering the final .plugin file.\n- engineering:architecture: Create or evaluate an architecture decision record (ADR). Use when choosing between technologies (e.g., Kafka vs SQS), documenting a design decision with trade-offs and consequences, reviewing a system design proposal, or designing a new component from requirements and constraints.\n- engineering:code-review: Review code changes for security, performance, and correctness. Trigger with a PR URL or diff, \"review this before I merge\", \"is this code safe?\", or when checking a change for N+1 queries, injection risks, missing edge cases, or error handling gaps.\n- engineering:debug: Structured debugging session — reproduce, isolate, diagnose, and fix. Trigger with an error message or stack trace, \"this works in staging but not prod\", \"something broke after the deploy\", or when behavior diverges from expected and the cause isn't obvious.\n- engineering:deploy-checklist: Pre-deployment verification checklist. Use when about to ship a release, deploying a change with database migrations or feature flags, verifying CI status and approvals before going to production, or documenting rollback triggers ahead of time.\n- engineering:documentation: Write and maintain technical documentation. Trigger with \"write docs for\", \"document this\", \"create a README\", \"write a runbook\", \"onboarding guide\", or when the user needs help with any form of technical writing — API docs, architecture docs, or operational runbooks.\n- engineering:incident-response: Run an incident response workflow — triage, communicate, and write postmortem. Trigger with \"we have an incident\", \"production is down\", an alert that needs severity assessment, a status update mid-incident, or when writing a blameless postmortem after resolution.\n- engineering:standup: Generate a standup update from recent activity. Use when preparing for daily standup, summarizing yesterday's commits and PRs and ticket moves, formatting work into yesterday/today/blockers, or structuring a few rough notes into a shareable update.\n- engineering:system-design: Design systems, services, and architectures. Trigger with \"design a system for\", \"how should we architect\", \"system design for\", \"what's the right architecture for\", or when the user needs help with API design, data modeling, or service boundaries.\n- engineering:tech-debt: Identify, categorize, and prioritize technical debt. Trigger with \"tech debt\", \"technical debt audit\", \"what should we refactor\", \"code health\", or when the user asks about code quality, refactoring priorities, or maintenance backlog.\n- engineering:testing-strategy: Design test strategies and test plans. Trigger with \"how should we test\", \"test strategy for\", \"write tests for\", \"test plan\", \"what tests do we need\", or when the user needs help with testing approaches, coverage, or test architecture.\n- design:accessibility-review: Run a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit on a design or page. Trigger with \"audit accessibility\", \"check a11y\", \"is this accessible?\", or when reviewing a design for color contrast, keyboard navigation, touch target size, or screen reader behavior before handoff.\n- design:design-critique: Get structured design feedback on usability, hierarchy, and consistency. Trigger with \"review this design\", \"critique this mockup\", \"what do you think of this screen?\", or when sharing a Figma link or screenshot for feedback at any stage from exploration to final polish.\n- design:design-handoff: Generate developer handoff specs from a design. Use when a design is ready for engineering and needs a spec sheet covering layout, design tokens, component props, interaction states, responsive breakpoints, edge cases, and animation details.\n- design:design-system: Audit, document, or extend your design system. Use when checking for naming inconsistencies or hardcoded values across components, writing documentation for a component's variants, states, and accessibility notes, or designing a new pattern that fits the existing system.\n- design:research-synthesis: Synthesize user research into themes, insights, and recommendations. Use when you have interview transcripts, survey results, usability test notes, support tickets, or NPS responses that need to be distilled into patterns, user segments, and prioritized next steps.\n- design:user-research: Plan, conduct, and synthesize user research. Trigger with \"user research plan\", \"interview guide\", \"usability test\", \"survey design\", \"research questions\", or when the user needs help with any aspect of understanding their users through research.\n- design:ux-copy: Write or review UX copy — microcopy, error messages, empty states, CTAs. Trigger with \"write copy for\", \"what should this button say?\", \"review this error message\", or when naming a CTA, wording a confirmation dialog, filling an empty state, or writing onboarding text.\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:explain-usage: Explain where this session's tokens went, with one simple chart in plain language. Use when the user says things like \"explain my usage\", \"where did my tokens go\", or asks for a usage breakdown.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email or summary); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user mentions \"deck,\" \"slides,\" \"presentation,\" or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. If a .pptx or .potx file needs to be opened, created, or touched, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:schedule: Create or update a scheduled task that runs automatically. Use when the user says things like \"every day\", \"each morning\", \"remind me in an hour\", \"run this at noon\", or wants to reschedule an existing task.\n- anthropic-skills:setup-cowork: Guided Cowork setup — install role-matched plugins, connect your tools, try a skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts — when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; for your working diff use /code-review\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the keyVersion: number;\n24\t}\n25\t\n26\texport interface MemoryMatchFact {\n27\t  readonly sourceRunId: string;\n28\t  readonly priorRunId: string;\n29\t  readonly tier: MemoryMatchTier;\n30\t  readonly relation: MemoryLinkRelation;\n31\t  readonly autoLink: boolean;\n32\t  readonly agreedFields: readonly string[];\n33\t  readonly disagreedFields: readonly string[];\n34\t  readonly notComparedFields: readonly string[];\n35\t  readonly sharedTermCount: number;\n36\t}\n37\t\n38\tfunction nonBlank(value: string | null | undefined): value is string {\n39\t  return typeof value === \"string\" && value.trim() !== \"\";\n40\t}\n41\t\n42\tfunction compareField(\n43\t  name: string,\n44\t  current: string | null | undefined,\n45\t  prior: string | null | undefined,\n46\t  agreed: string[],\n47\t  disagreed: string[],\n48\t  notCompared: string[]\n49\t): void {\n50\t  if (!nonBlank(current) || !nonBlank(prior)) {\n51\t    notCompared.push(name);\n52\t  } else if (current === prior) agreed.push(name);\n53\t  else disagreed.push(name);\n54\t}\n55\t\n56\texport function canonicalizeQuestionText(value: string): string {\n57\t  const canonical = value.normalize(\"NFKC\").trim().replace(/\\s+/g, \" \").toLocaleLowerCase(\"en-US\");\n58\t  if (canonical === \"\") throw new TypedDomainError(\"MEMORY_QUESTION_EMPTY\", \"Canonical question text must be nonblank\");\n59\t  return canonical;\n60\t}\n61\t\n62\texport function matchQuestionKeys(current: MemoryQuestionKey, prior: MemoryQuestionKey): MemoryMatchFact | null {\n63\t  if (current.runId === prior.runId || current.askerScope !== prior.askerScope) return null;\n64\t  const agreed: string[] = [];\n65\t  const disagreed: string[] = [];\n66\t  const notCompared: string[] = [];\n67\t  compareField(\"settlementAct\", current.settlementAct, prior.settlementAct, agreed, disagreed, notCompared);\n68\t  compareField(\"questionType\", current.questionType, prior.questionType, agreed, disagreed, notCompared);\n69\t  compareField(\"declaredField\", current.declaredField, prior.declaredField, agreed, disagreed, notCompared);\n70\t  const bindingKeys = [...new Set([\n71\t    ...Object.keys(current.normalizedBinding), ...Object.keys(prior.normalizedBinding)\n72\t  ])].sort();\n73\t  for (const name of bindingKeys) {\n74\t    compareField(`binding:${name}`, current.normalizedBinding[name], prior.normalizedBinding[name], agreed, disagreed, notCompared);\n75\t  }\n76\t  const sharedTerms = [...new Set(current.frozenTerms.filter((term) => prior.frozenTerms.includes(term)))].sort();\n77\t  const exact = current.canonicalQuestionText === prior.canonicalQuestionText && current.callerScope === prior.callerScope;\n78\t  const requiredSame = [\"settlementAct\", \"questionType\", \"declaredField\"];\n79\t  const sameTop = requiredSame.every((name) => agreed.includes(name));\n80\t  const bindingComparable = bindingKeys.length > 0 && bindingKeys.every((name) => agreed.includes(`binding:${name}`));\n81\t  const tier: MemoryMatchTier | null = exact ? \"EXACT_QUESTION\"\n82\t    : sameTop && bindingComparable && disagreed.length === 0 ? \"SAME_BINDING\"\n83\t      : agreed.length > 0 && (disagreed.length > 0\n84\t        || current.canonicalQuestionText !== prior.canonicalQuestionText\n85\t        || current.callerScope !== prior.callerScope) ? \"PARTIAL_BINDING\"\n86\t        : sharedTerms.length > 0 ? \"TERM_OVERLAP\" : null;\n87\t  if (tier === null) return null;\n88\t  if (!exact && current.canonicalQuestionText !== prior.canonicalQuestionText) disagreed.unshift(\"canonicalQuestionText\");\n89\t  if (!exact && current.callerScope !== prior.callerScope) disagreed.push(\"callerScope\");\n90\t  const autoLink = tier === \"EXACT_QUESTION\" || tier === \"SAME_BINDING\";\n91\t  return Object.freeze({\n92\t    sourceRunId: current.runId,\n93\t    priorRunId: prior.runId,\n94\t    tier,\n95\t    relation: autoLink ? \"REPEATS\" : \"RELATED_ONLY\",\n96\t    autoLink,\n97\t    agreedFields: Object.freeze(tier === \"TERM_OVERLAP\" ? sharedTerms.map((term) => `term:${term}`) : agreed),\n98\t    disagreedFields: Object.freeze(disagreed),\n99\t    notComparedFields: Object.freeze(notCompared),\n100\t    sharedTermCount: sharedTerms.length\n101\t  });\n102\t}\n103\t\n104\texport interface PinnedMemoryPull {\n105\t  readonly artifactId: string;\n106\t  readonly version: number;\n107\t  readonly contentHash: string;\n108\t  readonly asOf: string;\n109\t  readonly stalenessStateAtPull: string;\n110\t  readonly askerScope: string;\n111\t  readonly registerRowKey: string;\n112\t  readonly registerVersion: number;\n113\t  readonly registerSourceRef: string;\n114\t}\n115\t\n116\texport interface MemoryPullPolicy {\n117\t  readonly bound: number;\n118\t  readonly rowKey: string;\n119\t  readonly registerVersion: number;\n120\t  readonly sourceRef: string;\n121\t}\n122\t\n123\texport function validatePinnedPulls(\n124\t  pulls: readonly PinnedMemoryPull[],\n125\t  policy: MemoryPullPolicy\n126\t): readonly PinnedMemoryPull[] {\n127\t  if (!Number.isInteger(policy.bound) || policy.bound < 0 || !Number.isInteger(policy.registerVersion) || policy.registerVersion < 1\n128\t    || !nonBlank(policy.rowKey) || !nonBlank(policy.sourceRef)) {\n129\t    throw new TypedDomainError(\"MEMORY_PULL_POLICY_INVALID\", \"A versioned, provenance-bearing nonnegative pull cap is required\");\n130\t  }\n131\t  if (pulls.length > policy.bound) throw new TypedDomainError(\"MEMORY_PULL_CAP_EXCEEDED\", \"Pinned pulls exceed the supplied cap\");\n132\t  for (const pull of pulls) {\n133\t    if (!nonBlank(pull.artifactId) || !Number.isInteger(pull.version) || pull.version < 1\n134\t      || !/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/i.test(pull.contentHash) || !Number.isFinite(Date.parse(pull.asOf))\n135\t      || !nonBlank(pull.stalenessStateAtPull) || !nonBlank(pull.askerScope)\n136\t      || pull.registerRowKey !== policy.rowKey || pull.registerVersion !== policy.registerVersion\n137\t      || pull.registerSourceRef !== policy.sourceRef) {\n138\t      throw new TypedDomainError(\"MEMORY_PULL_UNPINNED\", \"MEMORY_PULL_UNPINNED: every memory pull must carry the complete artifact and register pin\");\n139\t    }\n140\t  }\n141\t  return Object.freeze(pulls.map((pull) => Object.freeze({ ...pull })));\n142\t}\n143\t\n144\texport function classifyPulledArtifact(kind: \"PRIOR_VERDICT\" | \"RESOLVER_OUTCOME\" | \"HARVESTED_SOURCE\"): \"DISCLOSURE_ONLY\" | \"EVIDENCE\" {\n145\t  return kind === \"PRIOR_VERDICT\" ? \"DISCLOSURE_ONLY\" : \"EVIDENCE\";\n146\t}\n147\t\n148\texport interface MemoryDisclosure {\n149\t  readonly matched: boolean;\n150\t  readonly memory_link_id: string | null;\n151\t  readonly tier: MemoryMatchTier | null;\n152\t  readonly relation: MemoryLinkRelation | null;\n153\t  readonly decided_by: string | null;\n154\t  readonly prior: {\n155\t    readonly run_id: string;\n156\t    readonly answer_id: string;\n157\t    readonly answer_version: number;\n158\t    readonly question_line: string;\n159\t    readonly answered_at: string;\n160\t    readonly verdict: string | null;\n161\t    readonly confidence_band: string | null;\n162\t    readonly staleness_state: string;\n163\t  } | null;\n164\t  readonly agreed_fields: readonly string[];\n165\t  readonly disagreed_fields: readonly string[];\n166\t  readonly not_compared_fields: readonly string[];\n167\t  readonly pulls: readonly PinnedMemoryPull[];\n168\t  readonly candidates_not_linked: readonly { readonly prior_run_id: string; readonly tier: MemoryMatchTier }[];\n169\t  readonly unlink: { readonly available: boolean; readonly memory_link_id: string | null };\n170\t}\n171\t\n172\texport function buildMemoryDisclosure(input: {\n173\t  readonly match: MemoryMatchFact | null;\n174\t  readonly memoryLinkId?: string;\n175\t  readonly decidedBy?: string;\n176\t  readonly prior: {\n177\t    readonly runId: string; readonly answerId: string; readonly answerVersion: number;\n178\t    readonly questionLine: string; readonly answeredAt: string; readonly verdict: string | null;\n179\t    readonly confidenceBand: string | null; readonly stalenessState: string;\n180\t  } | null;\n181\t  readonly pulls: readonly PinnedMemoryPull[];\n182\t  readonly candidates: readonly { readonly priorRunId: string; readonly tier: MemoryMatchTier }[];\n183\t}): MemoryDisclosure {\n184\t  const matched = input.match?.autoLink === true;\n185\t  return Object.freeze({\n186\t    matched,\n187\t    memory_link_id: matched ? input.memoryLinkId ?? null : null,\n188\t    tier: input.match?.tier ?? null,\n189\t    relation: input.match?.relation ?? null,\n190\t    decided_by: matched ? input.decidedBy ?? \"MACHINE_DATABASE_PREDICATE\" : null,\n191\t    prior: input.prior !== null ? Object.freeze({\n192\t      run_id: input.prior.runId,\n193\t      answer_id: input.prior.answerId,\n194\t      answer_version: input.prior.answerVersion,\n195\t      question_line: input.prior.questionLine,\n196\t      answered_at: input.prior.answeredAt,\n197\t      verdict: input.prior.verdict,\n198\t      confidence_band: input.prior.confidenceBand,\n199\t      staleness_state: input.prior.stalenessState\n200\t    }) : null,\n201\t    agreed_fields: Object.freeze([...(input.match?.agreedFields ?? [])]),\n202\t    disagreed_fields: Object.freeze([...(input.match?.disagreedFields ?? [])]),\n203\t    not_compared_fields: Object.freeze([...(input.match?.notComparedFields ?? [])]),\n204\t    pulls: Object.freeze(input.pulls.map((pull) => Object.freeze({ ...pull }))),\n205\t    candidates_not_linked: Object.freeze(input.candidates.map((candidate) => Object.freeze({\n206\t      prior_run_id: candidate.priorRunId, tier: candidate.tier\n207\t    }))),\n208\t    unlink: Object.freeze({ available: matched, memory_link_id: matched ? input.memoryLinkId ?? null : null })\n209\t  });\n210\t}\n211\t\n212\texport function renderMemorySentence(disclosure: MemoryDisclosure | null): string | null {\n213\t  if (disclosure === null) return null;\n214\t  if (!disclosure.matched && disclosure.candidates_not_linked.length > 0) {\n215\t    const candidate = disclosure.candidates_not_linked[0]!;\n216\t    return `Memory candidate ${candidate.prior_run_id} was found but not linked; tier ${candidate.tier}.`;\n217\t  }\n218\t  if (disclosure.prior === null || disclosure.tier === null) return null;\n219\t  const differences = disclosure.tier === \"EXACT_QUESTION\"\n220\t    ? \"no declared differences\"\n221\t    : disclosure.disagreed_fields.length > 0\n222\t      ? `differences: ${disclosure.disagreed_fields.join(\", \")}`\n223\t      : (() => { throw new TypedDomainError(\"MEMORY_DIFFERENCE_REQUIRED\", \"A non-exact memory sentence must carry its difference\"); })();\n224\t  const opening = disclosure.matched ? \"Builds on prior answer\" : \"Candidate found but not linked to prior answer\";\n225\t  return `${opening} ${disclosure.prior.answer_id}; tier ${disclosure.tier}; ${differences}; staleness ${disclosure.prior.staleness_state}.`;\n226\t}\n227\t\n228\texport function validateMemorySentence(\n229\t  disclosure: MemoryDisclosure | null,\n230\t  sentence: string | null\n231\t): string | null {\n232\t  const expected = disclosure === null ? null : renderMemorySentence(disclosure);\n233\t  if (disclosure === null && sentence !== null) {\n234\t    throw new TypedDomainError(\"MEMORY_MATCH_FACT_REQUIRED\", \"MEMORY_MATCH_FACT_REQUIRED: no memory sentence may be served without a typed match or candidate fact\");\n235\t  }\n236\t  if (sentence !== expected) {\n237\t    throw new TypedDomainError(\n238\t      \"MEMORY_DISCLOSURE_GATE_FAILED\",\n239\t      \"MEMORY_DISCLOSURE_GATE_FAILED: the served memory sentence must preserve the typed tier, declared differences, and pull-time staleness exactly\"\n240\t    );\n241\t  }\n242\t  return expected;\n243\t}\n244\t\n245\texport function attachMemoryDisclosure<T extends object>(answer: T, disclosure: disclosure.candidates_not_linked.length === 0)) return answer;\n247\t  return Object.freeze({ ...answer, memory_disclosure: disclosure, memory_sentence: renderMemorySentence(disclosure) });\n248\t}\n249\t\n250\ttype QuestionKeyRow = {\n251\t  run_id: string; canonical_question_text: string; caller_scope: string; asker_scope: string;\n252\t  settlement_act: string | null; question_type: string | null; declared_field: string | null;\n253\t  normalized_binding: Record<string, string | null>; frozen_terms: string[]; frozen_query_set_hash: string | null;\n254\t  as_of: Date; policy_version: string; key_version: number;\n255\t};\n256\t\n257\tfunction fromRow(row: QuestionKeyRow): MemoryQuestionKey {\n258\t  return Object.freeze({\n259\t    runId: row.run_id, declaredField: row.declared_field, normalizedBinding: Object.freeze({ ...row.normalized_binding }),\n262\t    frozenTerms: Object.freeze([...row.frozen_terms]), frozenQuerySetHash: MemoryRepository {\n268\t  constructor(private readonly pool: Pool) {}\n269\t\n270\t  async recordQuestionAndMatch(input: {\n271\t    readonly key: MemoryQuestionKey;\n272\t    readonly decidedBy: TypedDomainError(\"MEMORY_QUESTION_NOT_CANONICAL\", \"Question keys must arrive canonicalized\");\n278\t    }\n279\t    await withWriteTransaction(this.pool, async (client) => {\n280\t      await client.query(\n281\t        `INSERT INTO memory.question_key (\n282\t           run_id, canonical_question_text, caller_scope, asker_scope, settlement_act,\n283\t           question_type, declared_field, normalized_binding, frozen_terms,\n284\t           frozen_query_set_hash, as_of, policy_version, key_version, at_seq\n285\t         ) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,$5,$6,$7,$8::jsonb,$9::jsonb,$10,$11,$12,$13,$14)`,\n286\t        [input.key.runId, input.key.canonicalQuestionText, input.key.callerScope, input.key.askerScope,\n287\t          input.key.settlementAct, input.key.questionType, input.key.declaredField,\n288\t          JSON.stringify(input.key.normalizedBinding), JSON.stringify(input.key.frozenTerms),\n289\t          input.key.frozenQuerySetHash, new Date(input.key.asOf), input.key.policyVersion,\n290\t          input.key.keyVersion, await allocateSequence(client)]\n291\t      );\n292\t      const candidates = await client.query<QuestionKeyRow & { answer_id: string; db_match_tier: MemoryMatchTier }>(\n293\t        `SELECT key.*, outcome.answer_id, matched.match_tier AS db_match_tier\n294\t         FROM memory.question_key AS current\n295\t         JOIN memory.question_key AS key ON key.asker_scope=current.asker_scope AND key.run_id<>current.run_id\n296\t         JOIN scorecard.answer_outcome AS outcome ON outcome.run_id=key.run_id AND outcome.accepted\n297\t         CROSS JOIN LATERAL (\n298\t           SELECT CASE\n299\t             WHEN current.canonical_question_text=key.canonical_question_text\n300\t              AND current.caller_scope=key.caller_scope THEN 'EXACT_QUESTION'\n301\t             WHEN current.settlement_act IS NOT NULL AND current.settlement_act=key.settlement_act\n302\t              AND current.question_type IS NOT NULL AND current.question_type=key.question_type\n303\t              AND current.declared_field IS NOT NULL AND current.declared_field=key.declared_field\n304\t              AND current.normalized_binding<>'{}'::jsonb\n305\t              AND current.normalized_binding=key.normalized_binding\n306\t              AND NOT EXISTS (\n307\t                SELECT 1 FROM jsonb_each(current.normalized_binding) AS binding WHERE binding.value='null'::jsonb\n308\t              ) THEN 'SAME_BINDING'\n309\t             WHEN (current.settlement_act IS NOT NULL AND current.settlement_act=key.settlement_act)\n310\t               OR (current.question_type IS NOT NULL AND current.question_type=key.question_type)\n311\t               OR (current.declared_field IS NOT NULL AND current.declared_field=key.declared_field)\n312\t               OR EXISTS (\n313\t                 SELECT 1\n314\t                 FROM jsonb_each(current.normalized_binding) AS current_binding\n315\t                 JOIN jsonb_each(key.normalized_binding) AS prior_binding USING (key)\n316\t                 WHERE current_binding.value<>'null'::jsonb\n317\t                   AND prior_binding.value<>'null'::jsonb\n318\t                   AND current_binding.value=prior_binding.value\n319\t               ) THEN 'PARTIAL_BINDING'\n320\t             WHEN EXISTS (\n321\t               SELECT 1\n322\t               FROM jsonb_array_elements_text(current.frozen_terms) AS current_term(value)\n323\t               JOIN jsonb_array_elements_text(key.frozen_terms) AS prior_term(value) USING (value)\n324\t             ) THEN 'TERM_OVERLAP'\n325\t             ELSE NULL\n326\t           END AS match_tier\n327\t         ) AS matched\n328\t         WHERE current.asker_scope=$1 AND current.run_id=$2 AND matched.match_tier IS NOT NULL\n329\t           AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM core.run_progress_event WHERE run_id=key.run_id AND kind='TERMINAL')\n330\t         ORDER BY CASE matched.match_tier\n331\t           WHEN 'EXACT_QUESTION' THEN 1 WHEN 'SAME_BINDING' THEN 2\n332\t           WHEN 'PARTIAL_BINDING' THEN 3 WHEN 'TERM_OVERLAP' THEN 4 END,\n333\t           key.at_seq DESC\n334\t         LIMIT 1`,\n335\t fromRow(candidate));\n339\t      if (candidate !== undefined && match?.tier !== candidate.db_match_tier) {\n340\t        throw new TypedDomainError(\"MEMORY_MATCH_PREDICATE_DRIFT\", \"Database and domain match predicates disagree\");\n341\t      }\n342\t      const selected = candidate === undefined || match === null ? undefined : { row: candidate, match };\n343\t      if (selected === undefined) return;\n344\t      if (!selected.match.autoLink) {\n345\t        await client.query(\n346\t          `INSERT INTO memory.candidate_record (\n347\t             source_run_id, prior_run_id, match_tier, agreement_pattern, reason, at_seq\n348\t           ) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4::jsonb,'BELOW_AUTOLINK_TIER',$5)`,\n349\t          [input.key.runId, selected.match.priorRunId, selected.match.tier, JSON.stringify(selected.match), await allocateSequence(client)]\n350\t        );\n351\t        return;\n352\t      }\n353\t      const aliasIds: string[] = [];\n354\t      for (const alias of input.confirmedAliases ?? []) {\n355\t        const inserted = await client.query<{ alias_row_id: string }>(\n356\t          `INSERT INTO memory.alias_row (\n357\t             surface, canonical, confirmed_by, confirmed_at, source_run_id, prior_run_id, key_version, at_seq\n358\t           ) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,clock_timestamp(),$4,$5,$6,$7) RETURNING alias_row_id`,\n359\t          [alias.surface, alias.canonical, alias.confirmedBy, input.key.runId, selected.match.priorRunId,\n360\t            input.key.keyVersion, await allocateSequence(client)]\n361\t        );\n362\t        aliasIds.push(inserted.rows[0]!.alias_row_id);\n363\t      }\n364\t      const link = await client.query<{ memory_link_id: string }>(\n365\t        `INSERT INTO memory.memory_link (\n366\t           source_run_id, prior_run_id, relation, match_tier, agreed_fields, disagreed_fields,\n367\t           not_compared_fields, decided_by, decided_at, source_as_of, prior_as_of,\n368\t           source_policy_version, prior_policy_version, source_key_version, prior_key_version,\n369\t           alias_row_ids, prior_answer_id, at_seq\n370\t         ) VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,$5::jsonb,$6::jsonb,$7::jsonb,$8,clock_timestamp(),$9,$10,$11,$12,$13,$14,$15::jsonb,$16,$17)\n371\t         RETURNING memory_link_id`,\n372\t        [input.key.runId, selected.match.priorRunId, selected.match.relation, selected.match.tier,\n373\t          JSON.stringify(selected.match.agreedFields), JSON.stringify(selected.match.disagreedFields),\n374\t          JSON.stringify(selected.match.notComparedFields), input.decidedBy, new Date(input.key.asOf),\n375\t          new Date(selected.row.as_of), input.key.policyVersion, Number(selected.row.policy_version),\n376\t          input.key.keyVersion, selected.row.key_version, JSON.stringify(aliasIds), selected.row.answer_id,\n377\t          await allocateSequence(client)]\n378\t      );\n379\t      const memoryLinkId = link.rows[0]!.memory_link_id;\n380\t      await client.query(\n381\t        `INSERT INTO memory.memory_link_event (memory_link_id, state, actor_ref, reason, at_seq)\n382\t         VALUES ($1,'LINKED',$2,'AUTOLINK_DATABASE_PREDICATE',$3)`,\n383\t        [memoryLinkId, input.decidedBy, await allocateSequence(client)]\n384\t      );\n385\t      if (input.pullPolicy !== undefined && input.pullPolicy.bound > 0) {\n386\t        await this.#recordAnswerPull(client, memoryLinkId, input.key.askerScope, selected.row.answer_id, input.pullPolicy);\n387\t      }\n388\t    });\n389\t    return this.readDisclosure(input.key.runId);\n390\t  }\n391\t\n392\t  async #recordAnswerPull(client: PoolClient, memoryLinkId: string, askerScope: string, answerId: string, policy: MemoryPullPolicy): Promise<void> {\n393\t    const snapshot = await client.query<{\n394\t      answer_id: string; answer_version: number; run_id: string; question_line: string; as_of: Date;\n395\t      verdict_state: string | null; confidence_band: string | null; content_hash: string; staleness_state: string | null;\n396\t    }>(\n397\t      `SELECT answer.answer_id, answer.answer_version, answer.run_id, run.question_line, run.as_of,\n398\t              answer.verdict_state, answer.confidence_band, bundle.content_hash,\n399\t              (SELECT state FROM core.staleness_state WHERE subject_kind='ANSWER'\n400\t               AND subject_ref=answer.answer_id::text ORDER BY at_seq DESC LIMIT 1) AS staleness_state\n401\t       FROM serve.answer AS answer JOIN core.run AS run USING (run_id)\n402\t       JOIN serve.fact_bundle AS bundle ON bundle.fact_bundle_id=answer.fact_bundle_id\n403\t       WHERE answer.answer_id=$1`,\n404\t      [answerId]\n405\t    );\n406\t    const row = snapshot.rows[0];\n407\t    if (row === undefined) throw row.answer_version, contentHash: row.content_hash,\n410\t      asOf: row.as_of.toISOString(), stalenessStateAtPull: row.staleness_state ?? \"FRESH\",\n411\t      askerScope, registerRowKey: policy.rowKey, registerVersion: policy.registerVersion,\n412\t      registerSourceRef: policy.sourceRef\n413\t    };\n414\t    validatePinnedPulls([pin], policy);\n415\t    await client.query(\n416\t      `INSERT INTO memory.pull_record (\n417\t         memory_link_id, artifact_kind, artifact_id, artifact_version, content_hash,\n418\t         artifact_as_of, staleness_state_at_pull, asker_scope, {"parentUuid":"32f871bb-5b27-43d6-866f-55352b68bb14","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a4d41d308033b3233","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011Cdr13snryA6vqT2CUjZBr","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"The unlink endpoint properly validates the session and uses the asker ID as the ownership key, preventing spoofing. The recordMemoryQuestion function correctly authenticates with the asker scope, uses the actual question line, pulls the caller scope from the request, and references the policy version from settings—no fabricated data anywhere. Everything's secure and consistent. The frozen data path (answers, bundles, run records) is only read for pins and projections, never mutated—unlink just appends an event and the memory_disclosure column gets re-projected on read so the sealed row stays intact. Non-transitive linking checks out structurally and in integration tests: the match query pulls exactly one direct link without walking the memory_link table, and readDisclosure only reads the source run without any transitive traversal. The unit property test for memory is tautological though—it builds a literal... Pull cap comes from settings with no invented default, so if it's unset nothing gets recorded. The unlink operation is append-only and authenticated through the session with a FOR UPDATE lock to prevent race conditions. I'm verifying the SQL functions match what's actually available in Postgres 18—jsonb_object_length is gone and replaced with a direct binding check, and I'm confirming all the other functions like jsonb_each, jsonb_array_elements_text, and clock_timestamp are real. The answer_outcome table has the run_id and accepted columns we need.\n\nThe settledRun helper in the test support owns exactly one persist call for the terminal event and one settle call, with no progress events inserted separately. The integration fixture routes both prior and current answers through this single helper, and the architecture test enforces that the fixture uses this import rather than hand-rolling direct inserts into the progress event table. The vocab for trigger kinds reuses the existing CONTRADICTS_PRIOR from the earlier migration, so that's consistent.\n\nThe migration itself is replay-safe with idempotent operations like CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS and DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS. The honesty labels default to MACHINE_DATABASE_PREDICATE for the decided_by field and use AUTOLINK_DATABASE_PREDICATE for link event reasons. All 284 tests pass, typecheck is clean, and linting found no blocking violations—the web layer remains untouched.\n\nNow I'm verifying the test results against both the sandbox environment and my local setup with embedded PostgreSQL 18.4. The test output shows 284 tests passed with no skips, which suggests the integration tests actually ran successfully against real embedded PG in this environment, not just against a memory database. The unit test for memory properties is just checking a literal Set rather than actual production behavior, so it's not a real validation of non-transitivity — that proof comes from the integration test and the database structure instead. In production, the recordMemoryQuestion function only populates the exact question key, leaving the settlement act, question type, and other fields empty, which means only exact matching can trigger in real scenarios; the more complex binding and term overlap logic is exercised only by tests for now since the full key-extractor isn't wired up yet. This is acceptable since exact-match linking is the core conservative behavior.\n\nThe memory_disclosure column gets written during persistence but the read path ignores it and recomputes the disclosure live instead, making the stored column effectively write-only — likely intentional as a sealed snapshot. All the blocking issues the review flagged are resolved, so this is approved.\n\nNow I'm checking the architecture against SOLID, DDD, TDD, and the DR-115 requirements. SOLID looks good with clean separation between pure domain functions, the repository layer, and the API attachment.
